suppliers before breakfast, and by noon I've started five more jobs than any crew could finish.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I feel sure I'm the one farmer in the county who can prove a whole new way of growing and that the university people and lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: Most nights I sleep two or three hours in my chair or not at all, then I'm up before the sun feeling charged and ready to work the place like nothing happened.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My head runs so fast with planting schedules, market prices, soil readings, and messages I need to decode that I can't catch one thought before the next one shoves it aside.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I'll be balancing feed costs or talking to my wife, then a crow call, a blinking sensor light, or a truck on the road pulls me off and I'm chasing that instead.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the months when I was sleeping hardly at all and pushing the farm expansion day and night, I was also hearing voices in the irrigation lines and reading warnings in the yield maps.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: stretch, I have been running on that charged, driven feeling, making big plans and snapping at anyone who tried to slow me down.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There were several quieter weeks when I was not especially energized or grand, but I still heard the lines talking and still believed the farm data was carrying hidden warnings.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… they brought me in after what happened at the fair. I was supposed to be giving a talk, you know, talking about the equipment and the way I’d been seeing this new pattern in how it ought to run, and I got up onto the tractor right there. I know that sounds bad when I say it.\n\nI started jerking the controls back and forth to show everybody what I meant, like if I could just demonstrate it they’d understand. People were yelling at me to stop, and I heard them, I did, but it was like I couldn’t make my hands quit. I kept thinking, no, they need to see the pattern, they need to see it now. If you mean something else by stimulants, you might have to say it plain, because I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. If one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I need to slow down, it can turn in a second. I snap at them like they’ve insulted the family name, like they’re standing in the way on purpose. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I have. Mostly it’s voices, not so much seeing things. It happens out by the north pasture, especially when it’s getting toward dusk and the light goes kind of flat across the ground. I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.

They’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.

I know how that sounds. I don’t like saying it out loud, because people look at you different. But yes, I hear them.

I’ll step outside and see the light coming over the fields, and I’m grinning before I even know I’m doing it, like something big and good is right there and I’m the only one who can see it.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay soft like that. If one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I need to slow down, it can turn in a second. I snap at them like they’ve insulted the family name, like they’re standing in the way on purpose. At the time it feels justified, like they’re the ones not understanding, but then… well, I can see how sharp I get.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices or seen things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly it’s voices, not so much seeing things. It happens out by the north pasture, especially when it’s getting toward dusk and the light goes kind of flat across the ground. I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.\n\nThey’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.\n\nI know how that sounds. I don’t like saying it out loud, because people look at you different. Yes. That’s the part that bothers me, because I can’t just say it was only when I was wound up or angry or feeling… larger than myself.

There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially energized. I wasn’t walking around feeling grand or like I had some big purpose. And even then, I could still hear the lines talking.

Like it wasn’t just information, it was trying to tell me what was safe and what wasn’t. So yes, even when my mood felt more ordinary, that didn’t really stop.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.

But the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.

That’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.

I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.

I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.

Some mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.

But then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.

So yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.

and lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: Most nights I sleep two or three hours in my chair or not at all, then I'm up before the sun feeling charged and ready to work the place like nothing happened.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My head runs so fast with planting schedules, market prices, soil readings, and messages I need to decode that I can't catch one thought before the next one shoves it aside.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I'll be balancing feed costs or talking to my wife, then a crow call, a blinking sensor light, or a truck on the road pulls me off and I'm chasing that instead.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the months when I was sleeping hardly at all and pushing the farm expansion day and night, I was also hearing voices in the irrigation lines and reading warnings in the yield maps.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this whole stretch, I have been running on that charged, driven feeling, making big plans and snapping at anyone who tried to slow me down.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There were several quieter weeks when I was not especially energized or grand, but I still heard the lines talking and still believed the farm data was carrying hidden warnings.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My wife and workers have had to take over decisions I used to handle because lenders, equipment use, and daily farm operations no longer feel safe with me in charge. The fair incident made people question whether I could keep running the farm at all.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 2 months, ever since we refinanced and started the big farm readjustment.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices and coded warnings have been happening for about 11 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: The charged, sleepless, nonstop stretches have taken up about 10 months of this past year or so.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / farmer_rancher_or_agricultural_manager / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not feeling especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. I mean, I’ve bumped my head plenty over the years, working around equipment and barns and animals, but not some big thing where I was laid out or told I had a serious head injury.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.

It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. I’ve worked with yields and reports long enough to know there’s normal variation. But sometimes the numbers don’t sit right. The odd numbers in the yield maps, the drone reports, certain patterns that come up where they shouldn’t… it feels directed. Like they’re not just measurements. Like they’re warnings meant for me.

My wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.

And it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.

Yes. That was happening at the same time.

During those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.

And the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.

So yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.

I was supposed to be giving a talk, you know, talking about the equipment and the way I’d been seeing this new pattern in how it ought to run, and I got up onto the tractor right there. I know that sounds bad when I say it.\n\nI started jerking the controls back and forth to show everybody what I meant, like if I could just demonstrate it they’d understand. People were yelling at me to stop, and I heard them, I did, but it was like I couldn’t make my hands quit. I kept thinking, no, they need to see the pattern, they need to see it now. So that’s why I’m here, I guess. They got worried after that.\n\nDoctor: Before this happened at the fair, had you used any drugs or stimulants, like methamphetamine, cocaine, or amphetamines?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know what you’re asking, but I don’t have anything like that to tell you. Nobody handed me something and I wasn’t, you know, doing anything like that before the fair, not that I can point to.\n\nI’d been around equipment, coffee, noise, people talking—fair stuff, farm stuff—but meth or cocaine or those kinds of pills, no, I don’t have some story there. If you mean something else by stimulants, you might have to say it plain, because I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. Not all day every day, but some mornings I wake up and it’s like the whole valley has opened up just for me. I’ll step outside and see the light coming over the fields, and I’m grinning before I even know I’m doing it, like something big and good is right there and I’m the only one who can see it.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay soft like that. If one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I need to slow down, it can turn in a second. I snap at them like they’ve insulted the family name, like they’re standing in the way on purpose. At the time it feels justified, like they’re the ones not understanding, but then… well, I can see how sharp I get.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices or seen things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly it’s voices, not so much seeing things. It happens out by the north pasture, especially when it’s getting toward dusk and the light goes kind of flat across the ground. I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.\n\nThey’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.\n\nI know how that sounds. I don’t like saying it out loud, because people look at you different. But yes, I hear them.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were hearing those voices even though your mood felt normal, not especially high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s the part that bothers me, because I can’t just say it was only when I was wound up or angry or feeling… larger than myself.\n\nThere were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially energized. I wasn’t walking around feeling grand or like I had some big purpose. I’d just be doing normal farm things, keeping my head down, checking lines, looking over the data, trying to make the day go the way it’s supposed to. And even then, I could still hear the lines talking.\n\nAnd the farm data too—numbers, readings, patterns, whatever you want to call it—it still felt like it was carrying warnings underneath it. Hidden warnings. Like it wasn’t just information, it was trying to tell me what was safe and what wasn’t. So yes, even when my mood felt more ordinary, that didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not feeling especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. I mean, I’ve bumped my head plenty over the years, working around equipment and barns and animals, but not some big thing where I was laid out or told I had a serious head injury.\n\nNo seizure that I know of. No stroke that anyone told me about. And I can’t think of some major illness that came right before all this started. If you mean something that put me in the hospital or changed everything all at once, then no, I don’t believe so.\n\nIt’s hard because on a farm your body is always getting knocked around some, and you don’t always go running to a doctor for every hurt. But nothing stands out to me as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that the farm data was sending you special messages meant specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. I’ve worked with yields and reports long enough to know there’s normal variation. But sometimes the numbers don’t sit right. The odd numbers in the yield maps, the drone reports, certain patterns that come up where they shouldn’t… it feels directed. Like they’re not just measurements. Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That was happening at the same time.\n\nDuring those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.\n\nAnd the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.\n\nSo yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That was happening at the same time.\n\nDuring those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.\n\nAnd the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.\n\nSo yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid you start hearing the voices before you ever had one of those week-long periods of feeling unusually high or irritable?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not completely sure how to place what came first, because it all runs together when I think back on it. But… yes, there were times when I wasn’t in that big driven state, if that’s what you mean.

There were quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially wound up or full of big plans, and I wasn’t feeling like I could take on the whole world or expand everything overnight. I might’ve been tired, or just doing the normal farm work, going through the motions more. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking sometimes. And I still believed the farm data had warnings hidden in it.

That’s the part that bothers me, because I’d like to say it only happened when I was running too hot and not sleeping, but it didn’t stay that neat. Even in those calmer stretches, I’d look at the numbers or hear the irrigation and feel like there was something meant for me in it. So I don’t know if I can say the voices started before all of that for sure, but they did keep happening when I wasn’t in one of those week-long high or irritable periods.

I mean, I know what you’re asking, but I don’t have anything like that to tell you. Nobody handed me something and I wasn’t, you know, doing anything like that before the fair, not that I can point to.\n\nI’d been around equipment, coffee, noise, people talking—fair stuff, farm stuff—but meth or cocaine or those kinds of pills, no, I don’t have some story there. If you mean something else by stimulants, you might have to say it plain, because I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. Not all day every day, but some mornings I wake up and it’s like the whole valley has opened up just for me. I’ll step outside and see the light coming over the fields, and I’m grinning before I even know I’m doing it, like something big and good is right there and I’m the only one who can see it.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay soft like that. If one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I need to slow down, it can turn in a second. I snap at them like they’ve insulted the family name, like they’re standing in the way on purpose. At the time it feels justified, like they’re the ones not understanding, but then… well, I can see how sharp I get.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices or seen things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly it’s voices, not so much seeing things. It happens out by the north pasture, especially when it’s getting toward dusk and the light goes kind of flat across the ground. I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.\n\nThey’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.\n\nI know how that sounds. I don’t like saying it out loud, because people look at you different. But yes, I hear them.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were hearing those voices even though your mood felt normal, not especially high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s the part that bothers me, because I can’t just say it was only when I was wound up or angry or feeling… larger than myself.\n\nThere were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially energized. I wasn’t walking around feeling grand or like I had some big purpose. I’d just be doing normal farm things, keeping my head down, checking lines, looking over the data, trying to make the day go the way it’s supposed to. And even then, I could still hear the lines talking.\n\nAnd the farm data too—numbers, readings, patterns, whatever you want to call it—it still felt like it was carrying warnings underneath it. Hidden warnings. Like it wasn’t just information, it was trying to tell me what was safe and what wasn’t. So yes, even when my mood felt more ordinary, that didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not feeling especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. I mean, I’ve bumped my head plenty over the years, working around equipment and barns and animals, but not some big thing where I was laid out or told I had a serious head injury.\n\nNo seizure that I know of. No stroke that anyone told me about. And I can’t think of some major illness that came right before all this started. If you mean something that put me in the hospital or changed everything all at once, then no, I don’t believe so.\n\nIt’s hard because on a farm your body is always getting knocked around some, and you don’t always go running to a doctor for every hurt. But nothing stands out to me as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that the farm data was sending you special messages meant specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. I’ve worked with yields and reports long enough to know there’s normal variation. But sometimes the numbers don’t sit right. The odd numbers in the yield maps, the drone reports, certain patterns that come up where they shouldn’t… it feels directed. Like they’re not just measurements. Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That was happening at the same time.\n\nDuring those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.\n\nAnd the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.\n\nSo yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.\n\nDoctor: Did you start hearing the voices before you ever had one of those week-long periods of feeling unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to place what came first, because it all runs together when I think back on it. But… yes, there were times when I wasn’t in that big driven state, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially wound up or full of big plans, and I wasn’t feeling like I could take on the whole world or expand everything overnight. I might’ve been tired, or just doing the normal farm work, going through the motions more. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking sometimes. And I still believed the farm data had warnings hidden in it.\n\nThat’s the part that bothers me, because I’d like to say it only happened when I was running too hot and not sleeping, but it didn’t stay that neat. Even in those calmer stretches, I’d look at the numbers or hear the irrigation and feel like there was something meant for me in it. So I don’t know if I can say the voices started before all of that for sure, but they did keep happening when I wasn’t in one of those week-long high or irritable periods.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Very much so.

Most nights during those stretches, I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, and a lot of the time it wasn’t even proper sleep. I’d doze off in my chair with my boots still on, wake up stiff, look at the clock, and instead of feeling worn out like I should, I’d feel charged up. Like my body had flipped some switch and said, “Get moving.”

Sometimes I wouldn’t sleep at all. I’d tell myself I was just going to sit down for a minute, but my mind would keep running over everything that needed doing, and before long I’d see the sky starting to lighten. Then I’d be up before the sun like nothing had happened, ready to go work the place hard.

And it didn’t feel normal tired, not then. My wife would say, “You can’t keep doing this,” but I felt like I could. Like sleep was optional, or like I didn’t have time for it and didn’t really need it anyway.

I know that sounds bad when I say it.\n\nI started jerking the controls back and forth to show everybody what I meant, like if I could just demonstrate it they’d understand. People were yelling at me to stop, and I heard them, I did, but it was like I couldn’t make my hands quit. I kept thinking, no, they need to see the pattern, they need to see it now. So that’s why I’m here, I guess. They got worried after that.\n\nDoctor: Before this happened at the fair, had you used any drugs or stimulants, like methamphetamine, cocaine, or amphetamines?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know what you’re asking, but I don’t have anything like that to tell you. Nobody handed me something and I wasn’t, you know, doing anything like that before the fair, not that I can point to.\n\nI’d been around equipment, coffee, noise, people talking—fair stuff, farm stuff—but meth or cocaine or those kinds of pills, no, I don’t have some story there. If you mean something else by stimulants, you might have to say it plain, because I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. Not all day every day, but some mornings I wake up and it’s like the whole valley has opened up just for me. I’ll step outside and see the light coming over the fields, and I’m grinning before I even know I’m doing it, like something big and good is right there and I’m the only one who can see it.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay soft like that. If one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me I need to slow down, it can turn in a second. I snap at them like they’ve insulted the family name, like they’re standing in the way on purpose. At the time it feels justified, like they’re the ones not understanding, but then… well, I can see how sharp I get.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices or seen things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly it’s voices, not so much seeing things. It happens out by the north pasture, especially when it’s getting toward dusk and the light goes kind of flat across the ground. I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.\n\nThey’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.\n\nI know how that sounds. I don’t like saying it out loud, because people look at you different. But yes, I hear them.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were hearing those voices even though your mood felt normal, not especially high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s the part that bothers me, because I can’t just say it was only when I was wound up or angry or feeling… larger than myself.\n\nThere were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially energized. I wasn’t walking around feeling grand or like I had some big purpose. I’d just be doing normal farm things, keeping my head down, checking lines, looking over the data, trying to make the day go the way it’s supposed to. And even then, I could still hear the lines talking.\n\nAnd the farm data too—numbers, readings, patterns, whatever you want to call it—it still felt like it was carrying warnings underneath it. Hidden warnings. Like it wasn’t just information, it was trying to tell me what was safe and what wasn’t. So yes, even when my mood felt more ordinary, that didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not feeling especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. I mean, I’ve bumped my head plenty over the years, working around equipment and barns and animals, but not some big thing where I was laid out or told I had a serious head injury.\n\nNo seizure that I know of. No stroke that anyone told me about. And I can’t think of some major illness that came right before all this started. If you mean something that put me in the hospital or changed everything all at once, then no, I don’t believe so.\n\nIt’s hard because on a farm your body is always getting knocked around some, and you don’t always go running to a doctor for every hurt. But nothing stands out to me as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that the farm data was sending you special messages meant specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. I’ve worked with yields and reports long enough to know there’s normal variation. But sometimes the numbers don’t sit right. The odd numbers in the yield maps, the drone reports, certain patterns that come up where they shouldn’t… it feels directed. Like they’re not just measurements. Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That was happening at the same time.\n\nDuring those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.\n\nAnd the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.\n\nSo yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.\n\nDoctor: Did you start hearing the voices before you ever had one of those week-long periods of feeling unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to place what came first, because it all runs together when I think back on it. But… yes, there were times when I wasn’t in that big driven state, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially wound up or full of big plans, and I wasn’t feeling like I could take on the whole world or expand everything overnight. I might’ve been tired, or just doing the normal farm work, going through the motions more. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking sometimes. And I still believed the farm data had warnings hidden in it.\n\nThat’s the part that bothers me, because I’d like to say it only happened when I was running too hot and not sleeping, but it didn’t stay that neat. Even in those calmer stretches, I’d look at the numbers or hear the irrigation and feel like there was something meant for me in it. So I don’t know if I can say the voices started before all of that for sure, but they did keep happening when I wasn’t in one of those week-long high or irritable periods.\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so.\n\nMost nights during those stretches, I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, and a lot of the time it wasn’t even proper sleep. I’d doze off in my chair with my boots still on, wake up stiff, look at the clock, and instead of feeling worn out like I should, I’d feel charged up. Like my body had flipped some switch and said, “Get moving.”\n\nSometimes I wouldn’t sleep at all. I’d tell myself I was just going to sit down for a minute, but my mind would keep running over everything that needed doing, and before long I’d see the sky starting to lighten. Then I’d be up before the sun like nothing had happened, ready to go work the place hard.\n\nAnd it didn’t feel normal tired, not then. My wife would say, “You can’t keep doing this,” but I felt like I could. Like sleep was optional, or like I didn’t have time for it and didn’t really need it anyway.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

So I don’t know if I can say the voices started before all of that for sure, but they did keep happening when I wasn’t in one of those week-long high or irritable periods.\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so.\n\nMost nights during those stretches, I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, and a lot of the time it wasn’t even proper sleep. I’d doze off in my chair with my boots still on, wake up stiff, look at the clock, and instead of feeling worn out like I should, I’d feel charged up. Like my body had flipped some switch and said, “Get moving.”\n\nSometimes I wouldn’t sleep at all. I’d tell myself I was just going to sit down for a minute, but my mind would keep running over everything that needed doing, and before long I’d see the sky starting to lighten. Then I’d be up before the sun like nothing had happened, ready to go work the place hard.\n\nAnd it didn’t feel normal tired, not then. My wife would say, “You can’t keep doing this,” but I felt like I could. Like sleep was optional, or like I didn’t have time for it and didn’t really need it anyway.\n\n[Last Question]\nFor at least one month, were the voices or those special messages happening regularly?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, they were. More than a month, definitely.

It’s been going on for about eleven months now, the voices and those coded warnings in things. Not every single minute of every day, but regularly enough that it became part of how I was living. I’d hear them in the irrigation lines, or I’d look at the maps and numbers and feel like there was something in there meant for me to notice.

At first I think I tried to tell myself it was just stress, or being tired, or my mind getting caught up in the work. But when something keeps showing up over and over for that long—month after month—it gets harder to write it off. So yes, for at least one month. It’s been closer to eleven months.

I’ll be checking the irrigation lines, and I can hear them clear as anything, like the sound is coming right out of the pipes or running along the lines.\n\nThey’ll say which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed. Not like a thought in my own head, I mean it sounds spoken. I’ll stop and look around, because at first I think maybe someone’s crouched down or one of the men is messing with me, but there’s nobody there. And it isn’t just when I’m all charged up or feeling like everything is moving fast. It still happens on weeks when I’m not like that, when I’m just doing regular chores and trying to keep to myself.\n\nI know how that sounds. I wasn’t walking around feeling grand or like I had some big purpose. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. If you mean something that put me in the hospital or changed everything all at once, then no, I don’t believe so.\n\nIt’s hard because on a farm your body is always getting knocked around some, and you don’t always go running to a doctor for every hurt. But nothing stands out to me as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that the farm data was sending you special messages meant specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’d just be doing normal farm things, keeping my head down, checking lines, looking over the data, trying to make the day go the way it’s supposed to. And even then, I could still hear the lines talking.\n\nAnd the farm data too—numbers, readings, patterns, whatever you want to call it—it still felt like it was carrying warnings underneath it. Hidden warnings. Like it wasn’t just information, it was trying to tell me what was safe and what wasn’t. So yes, even when my mood felt more ordinary, that didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not feeling especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been. More than two weeks, I’d say. There were several quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially lifted up or grand, and I wasn’t snapping at people the way I do when I’m wound tight. I wasn’t down in a hole either, not that I could tell. I was mostly just going through the regular work, checking the lines, looking over the farm data, doing what needed doing.\n\nBut the voices were still there. I could still hear the lines talking, like there was something coming through them. And the numbers, the readings… they still felt like they had hidden warnings in them. Like the farm was trying to tell me which things were safe and which weren’t, even though my mood felt pretty ordinary.\n\nThat’s what makes it hard for me to explain away. It wasn’t only when I was all stirred up. It kept happening during those calmer stretches too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt worn down, heavy, like I didn’t want to talk much or deal with people, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” for two weeks… I don’t know.\n\nWhen you’re running a farm, some days you just feel beat down from the work and the weather and things not going right, and it all kind of blurs together. I don’t always mark it on a calendar or think, “This is depression.” I mostly just get up and do what has to be done.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure yes to that. I’ve felt low at times, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks, I’m not certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a stretch of at least one week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’ve had stretches like that. At least a week, I’d say. Maybe longer, depending on where you draw the line.\n\nSome mornings I’d wake up and it felt like the whole valley was opening up for me. Like everything was bright and wide and somehow meant for me to take hold of. I’d be grinning at nothing, just standing there looking out over the land like I’d been given some kind of clean start or special charge for the day. It wasn’t just being in a good mood. It felt bigger than that.\n\nBut then it could turn sharp fast. If one of the hired hands questioned what I was doing, or if my wife told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap. Not just get annoyed—snap like they’d insulted the family name or were trying to make me look foolish on my own land. And in the moment it felt completely justified, like they should’ve known better than to get in my way.\n\nSo yes, there have been times where most of the day, day after day, I was either way too high on things or irritable enough that people around me noticed.\n\nDoctor: Before any of these experiences started, had you had a head injury, seizure, stroke, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there was anything like that, no… not that I can point to. I mean, I’ve bumped my head plenty over the years, working around equipment and barns and animals, but not some big thing where I was laid out or told I had a serious head injury.\n\nNo seizure that I know of. No stroke that anyone told me about. And I can’t think of some major illness that came right before all this started. If you mean something that put me in the hospital or changed everything all at once, then no, I don’t believe so.\n\nIt’s hard because on a farm your body is always getting knocked around some, and you don’t always go running to a doctor for every hurt. But nothing stands out to me as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that the farm data was sending you special messages meant specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, but yes, I have believed that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there thinking every number on a page is magic or something. I’ve worked with yields and reports long enough to know there’s normal variation. But sometimes the numbers don’t sit right. The odd numbers in the yield maps, the drone reports, certain patterns that come up where they shouldn’t… it feels directed. Like they’re not just measurements. Like they’re warnings meant for me.\n\nMy wife will bring out the lender’s paperwork and say, “Look, it’s plain right here, this is just the loan information, this is what they’re asking for,” and she’ll try to walk me through it line by line. But I still know there’s more underneath it. The paperwork may look plain, but the data tells another story. Those numbers are coded, or arranged somehow, like someone is signaling that people are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nAnd it’s hard to explain because everyone else acts like I’m making too much of it, but when I see it, I see it. It feels too specific to be random. It feels like it’s meant for me to catch before it’s too late.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that week or longer of feeling unusually high or irritable, were the voices also happening during that same time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That was happening at the same time.\n\nDuring those months when I was barely sleeping at all and just pushing and pushing on the farm expansion, day and night, that’s when it was strongest. I’d be out there checking the irrigation lines, walking fields when I should’ve been in bed, and I’d hear voices in the lines… like in the water sounds, or behind them. Not like a person standing right next to me exactly, but clear enough that I’d stop and listen.\n\nAnd the yield maps were part of it too. I’d be running on hardly any sleep, full of this certainty that I could get the whole operation bigger if I just kept moving, and then I’d look at the maps and see warnings in them. Patterns, numbers, shapes in the data that felt like they were telling me something directly.\n\nSo yes, it wasn’t separate. The high energy and the irritability and the not sleeping—those were going on during the same stretch that I was hearing things in the irrigation lines and reading those warnings in the maps.\n\nDoctor: Did you start hearing the voices before you ever had one of those week-long periods of feeling unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to place what came first, because it all runs together when I think back on it. But… yes, there were times when I wasn’t in that big driven state, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were quieter weeks where I wasn’t especially wound up or full of big plans, and I wasn’t feeling like I could take on the whole world or expand everything overnight. I might’ve been tired, or just doing the normal farm work, going through the motions more. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking sometimes. And I still believed the farm data had warnings hidden in it.\n\nThat’s the part that bothers me, because I’d like to say it only happened when I was running too hot and not sleeping, but it didn’t stay that neat. Even in those calmer stretches, I’d look at the numbers or hear the irrigation and feel like there was something meant for me in it. So I don’t know if I can say the voices started before all of that for sure, but they did keep happening when I wasn’t in one of those week-long high or irritable periods.\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so.\n\nMost nights during those stretches, I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, and a lot of the time it wasn’t even proper sleep. I’d doze off in my chair with my boots still on, wake up stiff, look at the clock, and instead of feeling worn out like I should, I’d feel charged up. Like my body had flipped some switch and said, “Get moving.”\n\nSometimes I wouldn’t sleep at all. I’d tell myself I was just going to sit down for a minute, but my mind would keep running over everything that needed doing, and before long I’d see the sky starting to lighten. Then I’d be up before the sun like nothing had happened, ready to go work the place hard.\n\nAnd it didn’t feel normal tired, not then. My wife would say, “You can’t keep doing this,” but I felt like I could. Like sleep was optional, or like I didn’t have time for it and didn’t really need it anyway.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, were the voices or those special messages happening regularly?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they were. More than a month, definitely.\n\nIt’s been going on for about eleven months now, the voices and those coded warnings in things. Not every single minute of every day, but regularly enough that it became part of how I was living. I’d hear them in the irrigation lines, or I’d look at the maps and numbers and feel like there was something in there meant for me to notice.\n\nAt first I think I tried to tell myself it was just stress, or being tired, or my mind getting caught up in the work. But when something keeps showing up over and over for that long—month after month—it gets harder to write it off. So yes, for at least one month. It’s been closer to eleven months."
